(2) **field DOUBLE-wrap** — `emitSampleFieldAt`/`emitSecreteToField` wrapped (x,y) PAIRS, double-wrapping a corner coordinate shared between two samples; now `emitFieldWrapCoord` wraps each axis ONCE. (3) **field sample-before-deposit ORDER** — JS sink-hoists pure values to cell-top so a field read sees the PRE-deposit field; the WASM `preEmitAgentValues` pass (the WASM analogue of the WebGPU one, with `AGENT_VALUE_NO_HOIST` excluding RNG/mutable-storage/array-producer/forEach-element nodes — field reads ARE hoisted) now hoists the pure non-volatile value cone to agent-loop-top, matching JS exactly. (4) **`i32.trunc_sat_f64_s`** (a new encoder const, additive) for the f64→i32 value conversions — a NaN/Inf intermediate (aggregate.max over an empty array → -Inf; an expression with sin/sqrt) made the trapping `i32.trunc_f64_s` throw `float unrepresentable in integer range`; the saturating version is NON-trapping (NaN→0 like JS `x|0`) and bit-identical to plain truncation for finite in-range values (so the verified parity is preserved).
- **Verified — JS↔WASM BIT-PARITY on ALL 8 agent samples** (`scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs` — two stores, plain-JS + wasmBacked, same seed + hash + external regions, behaviour-fn-level): **0 mismatches over 150 steps, 64 agents** on Drift Test / Boids / Chemotaxis / GoL-on-agents / Ant Necrophoresis / the 3 Tissues (the force pass is W1-bit-exact; the structural phase is target-independent CPU → full-pipeline parity by composition). `scripts/check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs`: all 8 GATE✓ COMPILE✓ INST✓. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. **Browser-verified** (the worktree dev server on :5180, cross-origin module import into the running Chromium): all 5 agent samples GATE+COMPILE+INSTANTIATE in the REAL browser WASM engine, and the Chemotaxis field bridge is **BIT-IDENTICAL** (browser JS engine vs browser WASM engine, 20 steps, 0 mismatches).
- **PERF — THE BOOST MATERIALIZES for heavy rules** (`scripts/bench-agent-behaviour.mjs` — a chromatography-in-agents-style HEAVY per-agent rule: nearby gather → 3 aggregates → a Lookup Table → 2 expressions → 2 compares → 2 conditionals, the BEHAVIOUR fn only): **WASM vs JS = 5.0× at 500 agents / 2.8× at 2000 / 2.1× at 8000**. So the user's hypothesis HOLDS: WASM pulls ahead of JS as per-agent RULE COMPLEXITY grows, exactly like the CA grid. The trivial Boids force loop was a wash (W1 — V8 JITs a tight monomorphic numeric loop near-optimally), but a heavy behaviour rule (megamorphic typed-array reads, many branches, a lookup table) is where the JS interpreter overhead shows and WASM's compiled bytecode wins 2-5×.
- **The FINAL `isAgentGraphWasmSupported` reject set — EMPTY (zero node bans).** The only clamp-to-JS is the structural per-node array-scratch-slot budget (`> AGENT_NEARBY_SCRATCH_SLOTS=4` simultaneous getNearbyAgents producers → JS, never silently corrupted) — a capacity gate, not a node ban. WASM has NO precision/mode exclusions (f64 + sequential), so median/uniform-random/toggle-indicators/async-attrs ALL work (unlike WebGPU's f32/parallel fundamentals). `setCellLooks` glyph + a behaviour `stopEvent` are the two documented JS-side carve-outs (no per-agent glyph SoA / no GPU-style stopFlag round-trip — neither sample uses them; both clamp via the behaviour-reachable gate).

### Phase F — PR7 G1+G2+G3: the WebGPU agent-loop COMPILER + force shader (branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The SEPARATE WebGPU agent-loop compiler — the GPU sibling of `agentWasm/compile.ts`. Emits a WGSL `behaviour` module (G1+G2) + a standalone WGSL force-pass module (G3) over a GPU agent SoA. **The per-step runtime wiring + `agentTargetOf` enable (G3-runtime+G6) are NOW DONE** — see the next section. The Boids agent loop runs end-to-end on WebGPU (live in-browser flock verified, polarization 0.998).
- **A SEPARATE, additive compiler** [src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/) — `layout.ts` (`computeAgentWebGPULayout`: the GPU agent SoA = 7 strided storage buffers for the Boids subset, under the 8-binding floor so no `requiredLimits` raise; SEPARATE from the WASM `AgentMemoryLayout` — the GPU never shares memory, the worker uploads/reads back), `compile.ts` (`compileAgentGraphWebGPU` + `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported`), `forcePass.ts` (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL`). Touches NO lattice/grid WebGPU + NO existing agent JS/WASM path → lattice + JS-agent + WASM-agent byte-identity BY CONSTRUCTION (`git diff --stat` = only the DEV harness).
- **G1+G2 — the behaviour shader.** `dispatchCells` 2-D tiling, `highWater` a CONTROL UNIFORM (not baked — baking forces a per-gen recompile), the alive-skip + `idx>=highWater` guard. Universal nodes via f32 WGSL-string emit (reusing the lattice `emitWgsl` + select-divide-guard); the ~12 agent-node WGSL emitters (getNearbyAgents hash stencil + forEach + getAgentOffset torus fold + getVelocity/getAgentPosition/getAgentRadius + applyForce + setTargetRadius + setVariable). Per-agent PCG RNG keyed by `idx` (the lattice grid model — statistical parity, NOT bit-exact). Front-end = the SAME `expandMacros → collapseReroutes → CSE` the JS agent compiler runs. **Reconciled to the current base** (the prior 019375f reference pre-dated the Generic Agent Platform): the gate + all var sites read `model.agentVariables` (NOT the cell `variables`); the Compare op reads `cfg.operation` (the StatementNode key) NOT `operator` (a latent reference bug that would have emitted `==` for every non-equality Compare).
- **G3 — the WGSL force pass** ([forcePass.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/forcePass.ts)): the GPU sibling of `runAgentStep`'s force loop + `agentWasm` `emitForcePass` — the 3×3 hash-stencil neighbour pass (soft-sphere + density, torus-wrapped, all-pairs fallback) → velocity Euler (momentum/maxSpeed/dtOverEta) → wrap/clamp into the appended GPU `xNext`/`yNext` fields → age + growth ramp. Its own `ForceControl` uniform mirrors the WASM `FORCE_PASS_PARAMS` ABI. **`xNext`/`yNext` APPENDED to `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS`** so the behaviour shader's field bases stay byte-identical (re-verified: 5780 chars, compiles clean with the new fields). 2D-only, f32. **Bond springs + division + the hash BUILD stay CPU/JS** (the GPU SoA carries no bond store; the gate excludes bonded/division/field models, so for the Boids headline the force pass is exact).
- **The gate** `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)` — TRUE iff Agents enabled AND not 3D AND EVERY (flattened) agent-graph node ∈ `AGENT_WEBGPU_SUPPORTED_TYPES` (the Boids subset, ≤ 4 `getNearbyAgents`, scalar-vars-only, no options-mode getRandom, every forEach array source = getNearbyAgents). Mirrors the WASM gate + a 3D rejection. (G6: `agentTargetOf` now widens to `'webgpu'` when this gate passes — see the next section.)
- **Verified**: tsc clean; `compileAll(Boids).agent.webgpu` emits a 5780-char shader with exactly the 11 Boids node types; **both** the behaviour shader AND `emitAgentForcePassWGSL` compile with **0 errors + 0 validation errors** on a real `device.createShaderModule`/`getCompilationInfo`; `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(Boids)===true`, `(Tissue/Chemotaxis)===false` (getCellAttribute/secreteToField + 3D clamp to JS). DEV harness ([compileHarness.ts](src/dev/compileHarness.ts)) gained an `agent.webgpu` surface.
- **THE SCALE BENCHMARK (the headline) — JS vs WASM, force integrator.** `scripts/bench-agent-force.mjs` (esbuild-bundled, DOM-free) times the force-pass hot loop (hash build excluded) at 2k/10k/50k/100k agents (customForces / Boids case): **JS vs WASM is a WASH at every scale — 0.99× / 0.90× / 0.98× / 1.03×** (JS steps/s 5590 / 866 / 99 / 33). V8 JITs the tight monomorphic loop near-optimally (consistent with W1's ~1.0–1.25×); the agent loop is O(N) via the spatial hash so it is NOT a JS bottleneck at interactive counts. **WebGPU is intentionally absent** — its per-step whole-SoA upload+readback (CPU-built hash) is a fixed overhead that dominates BELOW ~10k agents, so WebGPU for agents is a wash-or-REGRESSION at interactive counts and only possibly breaks even at the very largest counts (needs the wired runtime to measure). The milestone's honest conclusion: there is no agent-loop speedup to be had from a faster target at the counts that matter — the spatial hash already won that on JS.
- **STILL TODO (later):** G4 (the CPU structural round-trip → Tissue), G5 (the field bridge → Chemotaxis), PR7c (zero-copy / atomics) — all still clamp to JS via the gate.

### Phase F — PR7 G3-runtime + G6: the WebGPU agent RUNTIME (Boids end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The runtime that makes the WebGPU agent target SELECTABLE + RUNNABLE — the GPU sibling of `instantiateAgentWasm` + the WASM `behaviour`/`forcePass` dispatch in `runAgentStep`. Boids flocks end-to-end on WebGPU (live verified: velocity polarization `|Σv|/Σ|v|` climbs 0.01 → 0.998 over ~200 steps, 260 agents, 0 worker errors, agents render + move).
- **A SELF-CONTAINED agent WebGPU runtime** [src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) — its OWN device + buffers + the two compute pipelines (behaviour + force), SEPARATE from the grid's `webgpuRuntime.ts` (so a model can run a WASM grid + WebGPU agents, the Boids case). `createAgentWebGPURuntime(behaviourShader, forceShader, layout)` acquires a device, compiles + validates both WGSL modules up front, allocates 8 buffers, builds the two bind groups (behaviour: 7 bindings agentF32 rw / agentI32 r / agentAlive r / hashBins r / control uniform / rngState rw / agentColors rw; force: 4 bindings agentF32 rw / agentAlive r / hashBins r / forceControl uniform — DIFFERENT binding indices, each shader its own bind-group layout over the SAME underlying buffers), + the two pipelines. Helpers: `uploadAgentSoA` (pack the CPU AgentStore f32/i32/alive SoA into the strided GPU buffers each step), `uploadAgentHash` (the CPU-built CSR hash → the hashBins buffer; returns false on overflow → JS fallback), `uploadAgentControl`/`uploadAgentForceControl` (the two uniforms, field order MIRRORS the shader structs + the WASM `FORCE_PASS_PARAMS` ABI), `dispatchAgentStep` (behaviour then force in one command buffer, `dispatchCells(maxAgents,64)` 2-D tiling), `readbackAgentStep` (read the f32 SoA back + commit `xNext/yNext → x/y` + `vx/vy/radius/density/age` into the CPU store — the JS `swapPositions` analogue), `seedAgentRng` (seed the per-agent PCG ONCE at creation; the GPU advances it in place across steps, so re-seeding every step is NOT done — that would freeze the sequence). f32 + per-agent PCG ⇒ statistical parity, NOT bit-exact (the documented WebGPU constraint). 2D-only.
- **`runAgentStepWebGPU()`** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)) — the GPU sibling of `runAgentStep`'s WASM dispatch (ASYNC: the readback awaits a `mapAsync`). CPU prep (reset forces, build the spatial hash, prime the sync attr buffer) → upload SoA + hash + the two control uniforms → `dispatchAgentStep` → `swapAgentAttrs` → `readbackAgentStep` → the CPU structural phase (a no-op for Boids: the gate excludes bonds/division/field). Returns whether the GPU ran (false → the caller runs the JS `runAgentStep()` for that step — hash overflow / device failure are never silently wrong). The bond springs + division + the hash BUILD stay CPU/JS (the GPU SoA carries no bond store); for the Boids headline the GPU force pass is exact.
- **The step-batch async branch** (sim.worker.ts `step` handler): the sync `for` loop can't `await`, so a JS/WASM grid + WebGPU agents model (Boids) routes to an async COPY of the batch loop when `agentTarget === 'webgpu' && agentWebgpuRuntime` — it awaits `runAgentStepWebGPU()` per step, runs the cell `runStep()` synchronously (the grid is JS/WASM here), then the colour pass + `sendColors`. The pre-existing sync JS/WASM-agent loop is UNTOUCHED (the new branch is gated + early-`break`s before it). (A WebGPU GRID + WebGPU agents model would route through the existing `webgpuActive` async branch — but Boids uses a JS/WASM grid.)
- **`agentTargetOf(cfg, wasmSupported, webgpuSupported)`** ([centerBased.ts](src/model/centerBased.ts)) gained a 3rd arg: a `'webgpu'` config resolves to `'webgpu'` ONLY when `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)`, else clamps to `'js'` (the always-runnable fallback — NOT 'wasm'). The worker-side `resolveAgentTarget` (sim.worker.ts) is the safety net mirror: `'webgpu'` demotes to `'js'` if either shader is missing from the message.
- **Build/dispatch lifecycle**: SimulatorView's `compileAgentModel` resolves the target (`isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported`), compiles the behaviour shader (`compileAgentGraphWebGPUForModel`) + the force shader (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL`), and ships both WGSL strings + the layout dims (`agentWebgpuMaxAgents`/`MaxHashBins`) in the init + recompile messages (mirroring the WASM `agentWasmBytes` path). The worker `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded()` (async, monotonic build-token guarded — the orphan-on-reinit discipline) builds the runtime; `initAgents()` drops a stale runtime (a re-alloc invalidates it); reset/recompile rebuild. Any device/compile/dispatch failure nulls the runtime → the JS behaviour fn + JS force loop run.
- **The Properties radio** (PropertiesPanelContent.tsx) WebGPU agent option is now ENABLED (not `disabled`) + its hint reflects live support (`isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)`): "runs on WebGPU (the Boids node subset, 2D)" when supported, else "falls back to JS (bonds / division / field stay CPU)".
- **Byte-identity** (verified `git diff --stat` vs the compiler-merge base `8d7c919`): the lattice grid compilers (`compile.ts`/`wasm/`/`webgpu/`) + the agent JS/WASM compilers (`compileAgentGraph`, `agentWasm/`) + the agent WebGPU COMPILER (`agentWebgpu/`) are ALL UNTOUCHED — the only diff is `centerBased.ts` (the 3rd `agentTargetOf` arm), `PropertiesPanelContent.tsx` (the radio), `SimulatorView.tsx` (the webgpu compile path in `compileAgentModel`), `sim.worker.ts` (the additive webgpu-agent branch, gated on `agentTarget==='webgpu'`), + the new `agentWebgpuRuntime.ts`. So lattice + JS-agent + WASM-agent behaviour is identical by construction for every non-WebGPU-agent model.
- **Verified**: `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean; live in-browser (Boids, agentTarget `webgpu`, WebGPU available) — gate `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported===true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, shader 5780 chars; 260 agents flock (polarization 0.01→0.998 over 200 steps, stable over 400+ steps, 0 worker errors), render as cyan dots, the Properties WebGPU radio is enabled + selected.
- **STILL TODO:** G4 (structural round-trip → Tissue), G5 (field bridge → Chemotaxis), PR7c (zero-copy / atomics) — clamped to JS by the gate. A HEAVY-per-agent-rule benchmark to show a WASM/WebGPU win (the simple Boids force loop is O(N) via the hash, so no agent-loop speedup from a faster target at interactive counts — the upload/readback overhead means WebGPU is a wash-or-regression below ~10k agents; it only helps at very large counts or with much heavier per-agent compute).

### Phase F — PR7 G5: the WebGPU agent FIELD BRIDGE (Chemotaxis end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The closed agent↔grid morphogen feedback (Decision D-FIELD) now runs on the WebGPU agent target — **Chemotaxis - Aggregation** flocks-and-aggregates on WebGPU. Additive over the G3-runtime; the lattice grid + JS/WASM-agent + the **Boids WebGPU** path are byte-identical (the field buffers are created ONLY when the model has agent-accessible cell attrs → a no-field Boids shader stays 5780 chars).
- **Two conditional field bindings on the behaviour shader** ([agentWebgpu/{layout.ts,compile.ts}](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): `fieldRead` (binding 7, READ-ONLY snapshot of the cell field at step start — all agents read the same pre-deposit values = a true parallel snapshot, a documented difference vs the JS path's sequential partial-deposit read, harmless for diffusion) + `fieldDeposit` (binding 8, `array<atomic<u32>>`, an f32-bitcast atomic-CAS accumulator per op set/add/sub/max/min so parallel agents writing the same cell don't race). The 5 field-node WGSL emitters — `sampleField` / `fieldGradient` (central-diff) / `readCellsUnder` (r-disk aggregate via `fieldSampleBilinear`); `affectCellsUnder` (r-disk) + `secreteToField` (4-cell bilinear splat via `fieldDepositCell`) — mirror the JS emitters' 2D math (f32, statistical parity).
- **Runtime** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): the two buffers + `uploadAgentField` (snapshot → fieldRead, prime fieldDeposit with the current field) + `readbackAgentField` (deposit → the cell read buffer). The worker's `runAgentStepWebGPU` ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)) does the CPU round-trip: upload the field BEFORE the dispatch, read the deposit back into `readAttrs[id]` AFTER (gated on `fieldReadLen||fieldWriteLen > 0`) so the cell CA step incorporates it. `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` now accepts the 5 field nodes; the worker derives the field spec from `fieldSpecs` (= `cellFieldAttrsOf`; readWrite subset = `cellFieldWriteAttrsOf`), the SAME order the shader compiled against.
- **Live-verified** (in-browser, `agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true` + `agentTargetOf==='webgpu'`, shader 2258 chars (`secreteToField`+`fieldGradient` present); the field BUILDS and the 220 agents AGGREGATE (220 spread bins → ~93 clustered, local density 1 → 8, ~2.4× concentration), **0 worker errors**; **Boids re-verified still flocks on WebGPU (polarization 0.998)**. (Recovery note: the implementing session crashed on its final API response with the work uncommitted-but-correct; recovered + independently re-verified.)
- **STILL DEFERRED** (after G4 — most since DONE by the WHOLE-TARGET PORT + the FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT): the agent-array tier + `getAgentAttribute`, 3D agents on WebGPU, and the 3D field bridge are all DONE; a glyph setCellLooks is now a parity no-op (un-gated). Only PR7c (zero-copy/atomics) + `stopEvent` on the agent behaviour remain.

### Phase F — PR7 G4: the WebGPU agent STRUCTURAL ROUND-TRIP (Tissue end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The CPU structural phase (bonds / division eigensolve / death / auto-bond) + the division-event fn are **target-INDEPENDENT** (pure CPU/TS in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `runAgentStructuralPhase`/`runDivisionEvent` + [agentEngine.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentEngine.ts) `divideAgent`) and run unconditionally. So G4 = (a) emit the structural-WRITE nodes on the GPU as per-agent REQUEST-FLAG stores + (b) read the request buffers back to CPU before the structural phase, mirroring how G5 added the field round-trip. **Morphogenesis - Differential Tissue** (division + bonds + maturity differentiation) now runs on the WebGPU agent target. The eigensolve + the divisionEvent fn stay JS on every target (NO WGSL division shader).
- **Request buffers ride the existing `agentF32` (binding 0) — ZERO new bindings** ([agentWebgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts)): the 9 structural-request fields (`divideRequest`/`divideAxisX`/`divideAxisY`/`divideAsym`/`bondFormReq`/`bondFormL`/`bondFormK`/`bondBreakReq`/`killRequest`, `AGENT_GPU_REQUEST_FIELDS`) are appended to `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS` AFTER `xNext`/`yNext` (so the force-shader bases stay byte-identical), stored as f32 (flags are small ints / an agent-id+1, exact ≤2²⁴; the axis/asym are float). The behaviour-shader emitters ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts) `compileFlowNode`): `divideAgent`→`agentF32[drBase+idx]=1.0` + axis (`0.0` when unwired → the engine resolves the tension axis: `divideAgent` resolves it whenever the axis is non-finite OR `(0,0)`, so `0.0` is behaviour-equivalent to the JS path's NaN default — and MUST NOT be a NaN literal/bitcast, see the gotcha in Verified) + asym; `formBond`/`breakBond`→`(target|0)+1` + restLength/stiffness; `killAgent`→`1.0`.
- **User AGENT attributes on the GPU** ([layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts) `agentAttrIds`/`agentAttrBase`): each `agentAttrsOf` id gets its own `agentF32` run appended after the request fields. `getCellAttribute`→`agentF32[attrBase+idx]` (f32; int/tag/bool round-trip exactly); `setAttribute`→write back with `round(...)` for int/tag/bool. `computeAgentWebGPULayout` gained a 4th `agentAttrIds` param; the SimulatorView compile (`compileAgentGraphWebGPUForModel`, uses `agentAttrsOf(model)`) + the worker rebuild (`buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded`, uses `store.attrSpecs`) derive the SAME ordered list (the layout-match invariant).
- **Universal nodes Tissue's behaviour uses** (new emitters): `categoricalColor` (multi-output palette select, shares the lattice `readCategoricalEntries`/`readCategoricalDefault`); `setCellLooks` (per-agent packed `agentColors[idx]` = `r|g<<8|b<<16|a<<24`, PLAIN mode only — a glyph setCellLooks clamps to JS; the `__current__`/any mapping writes unconditionally since the agent loop has one viewer); `neighbourDensity`→`agentF32[densityBase+idx]`; `getBondDegree`→`f32(bondCount)`.
- **Runtime** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `uploadAgentSoA` zeroes the request runs each step (a fresh per-step request slate) + uploads the agent attrs from `s.attrRead`; `readbackAgentStep` now ALSO copies the f32 SoA back into the engine's CPU request arrays (`s.divideRequest`/`s.bondFormReq`/…, flags rounded to int), the agent-attr runs into `s.attrWrite` (int kinds rounded), and the packed `agentColors` (a 2nd `copyBufferToBuffer`) into `s.colors`. The worker's `runAgentStepWebGPU` reorders to **dispatch → readback (attrs→attrWrite) → swapAgentAttrs (→attrRead) → structural phase** (the swap MUST follow the attr readback; in async agent mode `attrWrite` aliases `attrRead` so the swap is a no-op and the readback lands directly in `attrRead` — Tissue is async).
- **The gate checks ONLY the BEHAVIOUR-REACHABLE node set** (`behaviourReachableNodeIds` — the `behaviourStep.do` flow chain + its transitive value cone): the `divisionEvent` + `agentInit` roots are compiled SEPARATELY on CPU/JS (target-independent), so a Tissue graph whose divisionEvent subtree uses a node the shader can't emit (e.g. an extra `setAttribute`/`expression` on a daughter) still runs on WebGPU. The compiler's defensive loop + the getNearbyAgents-slot assignment are likewise scoped to reachable nodes. (Mirrors how the JS/WASM compilers walk one root at a time.)
- **`migrateForHarness`** ([compileHarness.ts](src/dev/compileHarness.ts)) now runs `migrateAgentAttributeSplit` + `migrateVariableScopeSplit` (mirrors LOAD_MODEL) — so the dev harness sees the split agentAttributes (Tissue's `maturity` moves cell→agent), matching the loaded model.
- **Byte-identity**: lattice grid + JS/WASM-agent + the no-structural-node Boids/Chemotaxis WebGPU shaders are unchanged (the request/attr runs only EMIT when a structural / Get-Set-Attribute node is reached; the layout's bigger `f32Len` is a buffer-size change, not a shader-string change). The force shader reads only the leading static fields, whose bases are unchanged.
- **Verified**: tsc + `npm run build` clean; the Tissue WebGPU shader compiles (gate `true`, target `webgpu`; `maturity` read/write at `agentF32[46200u+idx]`, the `divideRequest` flag store + NaN axis, the categorical palette select, `agentColors[idx]=` packed write, `neighbourDensity` read all present); Boids + Chemotaxis still gate `true` / compile with no request emit; the engine structural primitives consume the request values (the `(0,0)` GPU axis → the eigensolve resolves a finite tension axis on a bonded cluster, division grows the store, bonds partition, daughter inherits maturity). **LIVE-verified in the browser** (`agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true`, target `webgpu`, behaviour shader compiles + builds with NO fallback; Tissue **GROWS 5 → ~305 agents** (bonds 5 → ~841, self-limiting at ~290-305 = contact inhibition), **maturity differentiates** (the categorical green-stem→magenta-mature coloring renders), 0 worker errors; Boids (0.998) + Chemotaxis (aggregation) re-verified still run on WebGPU (no regression — the structural emit is gated). **GOTCHA (the bug live-verification caught — the headless check could not):** the unwired divide axis MUST emit `0.0`, NOT a NaN — `bitcast<f32>(0x7fc00000u)` has no literal `nan` in the shader STRING (so a string scan passes), but **WGSL/Naga CONSTANT-FOLDS the bitcast into a NaN constant and rejects it** at `device.createShaderModule` ("value nan cannot be represented as 'f32'"), so the behaviour shader silently failed to build and **fell back to JS**. Any future "magic sentinel" in a WGSL agent/grid emitter must be a representable f32 (the engine's `non-finite OR (0,0)` check makes `0.0` the safe sentinel here). A passing TS-compile / shader-string check is NOT proof a shader RUNS — only a real `device.createShaderModule` (i.e. a browser run) is.

### Phase F — PR7 WHOLE-TARGET PORT: full WebGPU agent coverage (70 node types; branch `continue_agents`)
The full-coverage push — `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` now accepts the ENTIRE agent-graph catalogue (**70** node types after `setAgentType`'s removal) minus the genuine fundamentals, so a user picks the WebGPU agent target and models freely (the CA-grid end state). Touches ONLY the agent-WebGPU files + the SimulatorView/worker agent-webgpu wiring — **lattice grid (2D+3D, all 3 targets) + JS/WASM-agent byte-identity by construction** (`git diff --stat` = `agentWebgpu/{compile,forcePass,layout}.ts` + `agentWebgpuRuntime.ts` + the gated SimulatorView/worker branches).
- **Added emitters** ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): the rest of the agent-array tier (`getBondedAgents`), `getCurvature`, `forEachBond` (the ragged bond store); the agent setters (`setVelocity`/`setAgentAttribute`/`setAgentsAttribute`/`setAgentPosition`/`setAgentRadius` — `setAgentType` was **REMOVED**), `updateAttribute`; the **universal** value/flow nodes — `groupOperator` (sum/product/min/max/avg/count/`weightedRandom`), `groupCounting`, `groupStatement`, `switch`, `loop`, `valueSwitch`, `getModelAttribute`, `lookupInteraction`, `proportionMap`, `interpolation`, `colorScale`, `getColorConstant`, `arrayElement`, `arrayLength`, `getConstant` tag, `getIndicator`/`setIndicator`/`updateIndicator` (atomics); **array Local Variables** + `setArrayElement`.
- **3D agents** ([agentWebgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts) `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS_3D` + `forcePass.ts`): the z fields (`z`/`vz`/`forceZ`/`zNext`/`divideAxisZ`) appended only when `gridDepth>1` (2D byte-identical); the getNearbyAgents/force-pass hash stencil becomes 3×3×3 with the `(nbz·nBinsY+nby)·nBinsX+nbx` bin index; `getAgentOffset`/`getSelfPosition`/`getAgentPosition`/`getVelocity`/`applyForce` get the z axis + dz torus fold against `control.fieldD`; the Control + ForceControl uniforms gain `maxBonds`/`nBinsZ`/`binSizeZ`/`fieldD`. The 3D agent loop runs on WebGPU; the 3D RENDERER (gl3d spheres) is still Phase E, so 3D agents are headless-correct but not yet drawn.
- **New conditional GPU bindings** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `auxF32` (9 — model attrs + lookup tables, the SoA-flat upload), `indicators` (10 — `array<atomic<u32>>`), `bondStore` (11 — interleaved `[partnerId, restLengthBits]` per slot, stride `maxBonds·2`). **Declared + bound ONLY when an emitter REFERENCES it** (the compiler ships `usesBondStore`/`usesIndicators`/`usesAux`; SimulatorView→worker→`createAgentWebGPURuntime` thread them) — NOT merely when the layout reserved the region: a declared-but-unused storage global is stripped by Naga, so its bind-group entry mismatches the pipeline's reflected layout (**the GoL-on-agents bug** — `maxBonds=2` reserved a bond store the totalistic rule never touches). The runtime adds `uploadAgentAux`/`uploadAgentIndicators`/`readbackAgentIndicators`/`uploadAgentBondStore`; the SoA upload/readback cover the 3D z fields.
- **`preEmitAgentValues`** (the cross-branch WGSL scoping fix): the agent compiler emits values inline at first use, so a PURE value read in MULTIPLE sibling branches (an Ant Necrophoresis `readCellsUnder` tested in both an `if`-branch and its `else`) was declared block-scoped inside the FIRST branch → "unresolved value" at `createShaderModule`. The pass walks the behaviour value cone and hoists every PURE, non-volatile node (incl. the hoistable sub-cone under an RNG-tainted consumer) to function-top, so the value cache serves every branch; skips RNG / mutable-storage / array-producer / forEach-element nodes; multi-output nodes pre-emit each consumed output port.
- **`emitLogic` OR→AND bug** (the GoL-on-agents all-die): `LogicOperatorNode` stores its op UPPERCASE (`'AND'`/`'OR'`/`'XOR'`/`'NOT'`) but `emitLogic` matched lowercase → every `OR`/`XOR`/`NOT` fell through to `AND`. GoL's birth rule `(sum==3) OR (sum==2 AND alive)` silently became `(sum==3) AND (…)` = always false → all cells die. Now `.toLowerCase()`s the op. (Boids/Tissue/Chemotaxis use AND, so it stayed masked.)
- **SimulatorView ships the FULL `r.layout`** (not just the dims) + `usesI32Write` + the usage flags, so the worker binds/uploads against the EXACT compiled layout (no recompute mismatch) — the layout carries the universal-node region bases (auxF32/indicators/bondStore/3D z).
- **The FINAL reject set — only the documented fundamentals** (post the 3D-field + glyph close-out): (1) `aggregate`/`groupOperator` `median` + uniform `random` (no sort / per-cell-pick path — same as the lattice WebGPU grid; `weightedRandom` IS supported); (2) `updateIndicator` `toggle`/`next`/`previous` (order-dependent under parallel writers — same as the grid); (3) > 6 agent-array producers (the `var<function>` register budget — a CAPACITY gate, not a node ban). The orientation/variegation nodes never reach the Agents graph (variegation is grid-only); `stopEvent` on the agent behaviour stays deferred (a GPU stopFlag round-trip + the `_stopIdx` offset — matching the WASM agent path; no sample uses it). f32 precision bars NO node; async agent mode WORKS (Tissue is async; the agent loop is single-buffer). **Glyph `setCellLooks` + 3D field nodes are NO LONGER rejected — see the FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT below.**
- **GPU-LIVE-verified in the browser** (real `device.createShaderModule` + 0 validation errors AND the running worker on `agentTarget:'webgpu'`, 0 worker errors, NO JS fallback): **Boids 2D** flocks (polarization 0.999); **3D Boids** flocks (3D pol 0.023→0.982, z spans the 32-deep volume, force pass integrates z); **Chemotaxis** aggregates (occupied bins 174→106, sum-of-squares 318→670 ≈ 2× clustering via the field bridge); **GoL-on-agents** evolves like Conway (344→155→107 alive, stabilising into still-lifes/blinkers — the OR-fix); **Tissue** grows 5→274 agents / 745 bonds with maturity differentiation (7 distinct colours, self-limiting at contact inhibition); **Ant Necrophoresis** runs 0-error + matches the JS baseline. The lattice grid (Game of Life 2D + Life3D) compiles clean on all 3 targets (no regression).

### Phase F — PR7 FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT: WebGPU agents 3D field bridge + glyph setCellLooks (branch `continue_agents`)
The last two WebGPU-agent gaps closed (the FULL-COVERAGE mandate). Touches ONLY `agentWebgpu/compile.ts` (+ a defensive guard in `agentWasm/compile.ts`) — lattice grid (all 3 targets) + JS/WASM-agent + the agentWebgpu RUNTIME are byte-identical by construction.
- **3D field bridge (trilinear) — DONE + GPU-live-verified.** The 2D bilinear field bridge already ran on WebGPU; the layout (`fieldTotal = W·H·D`, `fieldReadBase`/`fieldWriteBase` strided by `fieldTotal`) + the control struct (`fieldD`) + the runtime round-trip (`uploadAgentField`/`readbackAgentField` copy the whole flat field array) were ALREADY 3D-correct — only the WGSL emitter MATH was 2D-locked. `emitFieldHelpers(is3d)`: a 3D model emits **`fieldSampleTrilinear`** (8-corner read, index `(z·H+y)·W+x`, z-axis torus/clamp) instead of `fieldSampleBilinear`; `fieldSampleCall()` picks bilinear/trilinear + threads the agent z. `sampleField`/`fieldGradient` (the latter emits the `dz` central-difference port in 3D — the node already exposes `dz`) sample trilinearly. `readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder`: the r-disk becomes an r-**SPHERE** (a layer loop + 3D torus-SHORTEST membership fold + the 3D field index, mirroring the 3D JS nodes; the 2D paths stay byte-identical). `secreteToField` = an 8-cell trilinear splat (weights sum to 1). `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` dropped the field-in-3D clamp. The mirror 3D math matches SampleFieldNode/FieldGradientNode/ReadCellsUnder/AffectCellsUnder/SecreteToFieldNode JS (statistical parity — f32/per-cell PCG). **GPU-live-verified** (3D Chemotaxis 32×32×16 on `agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, the behaviour shader (incl. `fieldSampleTrilinear` + z-splat) AND the 3D force pass compile **0 errors / 0 validation** on a real device; 0 worker errors, the 3D field BUILDS (chemical max ~4.5, 16368/16384 cells non-zero — the trilinear secrete spread through the volume) and the 220 agents span the full z depth (z 0.13..15.74).
  - **3D spatial-hash reserve fix (surfaced by the GPU run):** `agentMaxHashBinsForModelGPU` computed `nx·ny` only — it DROPPED the Z dim, so the 3D 3×3×3 hash (108 bins) overflowed the 36-bin reserve and the worker's per-step fits-check fell back to JS EVERY step. Fixed to `nx·ny·nz` (mirrors the shared `computeAgentMaxHashBins`); 2D (D=1→nz=1) byte-identical. Propagates through `r.layout.maxHashBins` → the shipped `agentWebgpuLayout` → the worker fits-check.
  - **WASM-agent 3D field is NOW PORTED — see "Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE" below.** (The old defensive `isAgentGraphWasmSupported` field-in-3D clamp is removed; a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with JS bit-parity.)
- **Glyph `setCellLooks` un-gated (parity no-op) — DONE + GPU-live-verified.** Key finding: the AGENT render path (`drawAgentsOverlay`) draws filled circles ONLY — NO glyph overlay on ANY target. On JS/WASM a glyph setCellLooks writes the per-AGENT glyph buffer, which for the agent loop is the **length-0 `GLYPH_NOOP` buffer** (a silently-dropped typed-array write) = a no-op. So a glyph setCellLooks is HARMLESS, not a fundamental — it was only the WebGPU gate that banned the glyph-without-background case. `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` dropped that reject; `emitSetCellLooks` is unchanged (writes the background colour when `setBackground`, and the glyph-only-no-background case early-returns = the SAME no-op as JS/WASM — NO per-agent glyph buffers added, so NO cross-target rendering divergence). Adding real per-agent glyph rendering would be a NEW cross-target feature (AgentStore/snapshot/render + JS/WASM/WebGPU parity), not a coverage gap. **GPU-live-verified**: a Boids model with a glyph (no-background) setCellLooks — the previously-BANNED case — now gates `true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, the shader compiles 0 errors (with NO `glyphCode`/`glyphColor` buffer references — the no-op confirmed), and Boids flocks end-to-end on WebGPU (polarization 0.81+, 260 agents, 0 worker errors).
- **Byte-identity**: `git diff --stat` vs `continue_agents` = `webgpu/compile.ts` (grid median/random) + `agentWebgpu/compile.ts` (3D field + glyph + hash-reserve) + `agentWasm/compile.ts` (the +10-line 3D-field guard). NO lattice grid (`compile.ts`/`wasm/`/`webgpu/encoder.ts`/`layout.ts`) + NO JS-agent compiler + NO `agentEngine.ts`/`agentWebgpuRuntime.ts` touched. **THE COMPILE MATRIX IS NOW FULL COVERAGE** — every per-target gate rejects ONLY the genuine fundamentals (WebGPU async-update-mode + order-dependent indicator ops [+ the agent-array-producer capacity gate]).

### Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE (the last WASM-agent gap; branch `continue_agents`)
The WASM-agent field bridge was 2D-only (`emitFieldIdx` = `row·W+col`), so a 3D-field WASM-agent model defensively clamped to JS (the one documented WASM follow-up after the whole-target port). NOW PORTED to 3D — a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with **JS↔WASM bit-parity** (f64). The WebGPU agent 3D field bridge (the prior close-out) is the structural model. Touches ONLY [agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) (+ the parity harness) — lattice grid (all 3 targets) + JS-agent + WebGPU-agent (compiler AND runtime) are byte-identical by construction (`git diff --stat` = `agentWasm/compile.ts` + `scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs`).
- **All 5 field emitters branch on `ctx.is3d`** (the agent loop is already 3D-aware — getNearbyAgents / getAgentOffset / the force pass), mirroring the JS field nodes (SampleField / FieldGradient / ReadCellsUnder / AffectCellsUnder / SecreteToField) bit-for-bit:
  - `emitFieldIdx(row, col, layer?)` — 3D index `(layer·H+row)·W+col` (2D `row·W+col` unchanged).
  - `emitFieldWrapCoord(cL, axis)` — axis selector `'x'`/`'y'`/`'z'` (z wraps/clamps against `_fieldD`); `pushFieldDInt`.
  - `emitSampleFieldAt(…, pushPZ?)` — 3D 8-corner trilinear via the SAME **NESTED lerp** the JS node uses (lerp_x → lerp_y → lerp_z). NB: a flat `Σ c·wx·wy·wz` is mathematically equal but NOT bit-identical to the nested form (different f64 rounding) — the nested lerp is required for parity.
  - `emitFieldGradient` — emits the `dz` central-difference port (the node already exposes `dz` in 3D).
  - `emitSphereLoop` + `emitTorusDeltaFold` — the 3D r-SPHERE scan: a layer loop + 3D **torus-SHORTEST membership fold** (the 3D JS uses the fold; the 2D `emitDiskLoop` uses the raw delta) + the 3D index. `readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder` dispatch sphere (3D) vs disk (2D).
  - `emitSecreteToField` — 8-cell trilinear splat in 3D (flat `rate·wx·wy·wz`, matching the JS flat splat; weights sum to 1).
- **The gate** (`isAgentGraphWasmSupported`) dropped the field-in-3D clamp — a 3D-field agent model now compiles to WASM.
- **Two parity bugs the comprehensive harness caught + fixed** (the gold-standard f64 verification surfaced both): (1) `emitSampleFieldAt` 3D first used a flat 8-term sum — bit-divergent from the JS nested lerp (fixed to the nested form); (2) `emitTorusDeltaFold` computed `-half` as `(half - 0)` instead of `(0 - half)` (WASM `f64.sub` is `a-b`; operands were reversed), so the `d < -half` branch fired for nearly every cell and added the full span to the delta → the r-sphere membership matched ZERO cells (read returned 0). Fixed the operand order.
- **Verified — JS↔WASM bit-parity on ALL 9 agent samples** (8 shipped + a SYNTHETIC 3D-field model built in-memory by [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)'s `build3DFieldModel`, exercising ALL 5 field nodes in 3D): 0 mismatches over 150 (+ 300) steps, 64 agents, incl. the readWrite `field_` arrays (so the 3D secrete splat + affect scatter write bit-identical cells too). The 8 shipped samples are unregressed; `check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs` GATE✓ COMPILE✓ INST✓ on all + the synthetic. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. The 3D-field model is **synthetic (in-harness)** — not shipped to the Models Library (the existing samples don't cover 3D field; this keeps permanent regression coverage without a library card). **THE WASM AGENT TARGET IS NOW FULL 2D+3D COVERAGE.**

### Production-hardening pass (branch `polish_agents` — full agents+3D audit, all findings fixed)
A six-agent review swept the agent engine, the three agent compile targets, the ~42 agent nodes, the 3D grid/renderer, the UI lifecycle, and the docs; every confirmed finding was fixed. Verified: tsc + `npm run build` clean; ALL 21 library models compile clean across JS/WASM/WebGPU (sweep via the dev harness); the 9-entry JS↔WASM agent parity harness = 0 mismatches; `check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs` all ✓. The load-bearing changes:
- **Agent state persists in `.gcastate` / Save-Project-with-grid** (was silently dropped — a hand-seeded agent board vanished on save/load). `SimulationState.agents?: SerializedAgentState` (+ `activeAgentViewer?`), base64-generic over the worker `AgentStatePayload` via `serializeAgentState`/`deserializeAgentState` ([fileOperations.ts](src/model/fileOperations.ts)); `applySimulationState` forwards it to the worker `loadState`; a state WITHOUT an agent payload re-seeds the agent layer (loadState else-branch) instead of keeping the old population. `AgentStatePayload` also gained `vx`/`vy` + the 3 sprite arrays (zeroed-then-loaded on deserialize — momentum models keep their motion; loading over a running session can't inherit stale slot velocities).
- **Async read-after-write hazards now cover AGENT roots on all three targets** ([asyncWriteHazard.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/asyncWriteHazard.ts) gained the agent reader/writer tables + synthetic `__agentVelocity__`/`__agentPosition__`/`__agentRadius__` keys): a behaviour `Set Attribute → read later in flow` reads post-write instead of a hoisted stale snapshot (async agent mode; division events ALWAYS — the division ABI's `w_` block now ALIASES attrRead, fixing sync-mode division writes being clobbered by the next step's `primeAgentAttrWrite`). JS pins via the existing volatile machinery; the agent WASM/WebGPU compilers consume the SAME shared `computeVolatileHoist` emitBefore plan (`ctx.hazardPinned`/`hazardEmitBefore` — emit ONCE at the LCA, NOT re-emit-per-use, which diverged on multi-use reads with a write between uses; the Ant pickup-then-drop shape caught it). Field reads stay a loop-top snapshot on every target (JS `routeValueEmit` forces them to CELL_TOP on agent roots so branch-sinking can't see a same-step deposit the WebGPU fieldRead snapshot can't). The agent WASM/WebGPU branch emitters also clear the volatile cache at conditional/switch/loop BRANCH boundaries (a getVariable-derived value cached from one branch left the sibling reading a stale local on WASM / an unresolved name on WGSL).
- **WASM agent emitter fixes** (all silent-wrong before): Compare `between`/`notBetween` (fell through to `==`), Math `%` (fell through to `+`; now host `env.fmod` + the ÷0 guard — `FMOD_FUNC_IDX` is module-scope), options-mode Get Random (fell into the float branch; now emits array-pick + Fallback, inputs resolved BEFORE the RNG advance), bool-mode Get Random (double i32→f64 convert = module VALIDATION failure → silent JS fallback), `filterAgents`/`joinAgents` `count`-port standalone consumption (threw → silent JS clamp; now the lattice count-dispatch pattern), pickRandomAgent resolves its input array BEFORE the RNG advance (chained-RNG-producer order), getConstant orientation-clamp/faceLabel parity. WebGPU agent mirrors: `between` + `%` + options-mode Get Random now EMIT (their gate rejects removed; `compareType === 'neighborIndex'` is the only Compare reject left).
- **setCellLooks viewer guard on the WASM agent behaviour**: a 16th `activeViewerIdx` i32 param + the compile-time `viewerGuardIds` table (compile result → init/recompile msg `agentWasmViewerGuardIds` → the worker passes `indexOf(activeViewer)`) reproduces JS's `_isV_` guard exactly (was an unconditional write — cross-target visual divergence on multi-viewer models).
- **CSE/loop-invariance hardening** ([accessorCSE.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/accessorCSE.ts) / [loopInvariant.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/loopInvariant.ts)): any node with a FLOW port is never a CSE source (two bare For Each Bonds shared a purity key → out-of-scope-const ReferenceError; two Create Agents merged handles → silent population loss); the agent engine-buffer readers (velocity/position/radius/nearby/curvature/field reads) + `createAgent` + the agent roots joined `NEVER_PURE_TYPES`; `createAgent`/`agentInit`/`agentOutputMapping` joined `NEVER_INVARIANT` (handle-consumer TDZ crash in the Init Event).
- **By-id reader guards on ALL THREE targets** (Get Agent Position abs+rel / Offset / Radius / Attribute + wired Get Velocity): `-1` (the empty sentinel — Pick Random Agent on an empty set) / out-of-range ids yield 0 instead of `undefined→NaN` position math (JS) or adjacent-memory reads (WASM); unwired reader ids default to `-1`. WASM = `emitAgentIdGuard` (ok flag + clamped safe id + `OP_SELECT`, eager-load-safe); WebGPU = `emitAgentIdGuarded` + `select()`. `forEachBond.currentLength` is torus-folded + 3D-aware on all targets (a seam bond read ≈ W − actual and "break over-strained bonds" severed every seam bond; WebGPU already folded 2D — JS/WASM caught up, all three gained the z arm).
- **Worker lifecycle fixes** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): the cell Init Event runs BEFORE the agent Init Event on load AND Reset (D-FIELD ordering — a Create-Agent rule that reads the field sees the seeded substrate); `runAgentStepWebGPU` resets `forceZ` (a JS fallback step left a permanent phantom z-force in 3D) and DEFERS mutation messages arriving during its awaited readback (`AGENT_GPU_DEFER_TYPES` + `flushDeferredAgentGpuMsgs` — a mid-step move/seed/paint was clobbered by the readback); `agentAddToWorld` only commits ids staged by THIS Init Event's Create Agent (ghost slots / free-list double-alloc); `currentAgentHash` is invalidated in `initAgents` + after the auto-bond rebuild (it reuses the same per-store scratch arrays); a throwing agent OM colour pass removes itself (was one error post per step); the `createAgent` msg carries `z`; daughter B inherits sprite state in `divideAgent`; the recompile message now ships `agentLayoutExtras`/`agentWebgpuLayout`/`agentWebgpuUsesI32Write`/usage flags (a live target flip to WASM rebuilt the store WITHOUT the extras the module was compiled against — the baked-offset corruption class); the agent Init ABI carries a trailing `_agentZ` in 3D (Set Agent Position's z write threw `_agentZ is not defined` — the canonical 3D spawn recipe crashed) + `zNext` syncs after init; the division ABI carries `_bondRestLength`/`_bondPartnerEpoch` (For Each Bond in a division event crashed on the missing param).
- **3D fixes**: `coords3d` is seeded (`coords` with dl=0) on the dimension flip to 3D AND on LOAD_MODEL, and the JS NI pre-pass `packCoord` falls back to `coords`+dl=0 like the worker (a 2D-authored neighbourhood in a 3D model packed all-INVALID_NI while the sim kept running it, and the slice editor showed 0 cells + clobbered on first click); the agent sphere pick maps back through the alpha-sort permutation (`agentInstOrder` in [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) — with Alpha blend on, kill/move/inspect hit the WRONG agent); `pick()`/`pickAgent()` respect the Layers Show toggles (no killing invisible agents); Ctrl+C/V/X is gated OFF in 3D (it silently corrupted layer-0 cells at a bogus 2D-projected position — a layer-aware region copy anchored on the brush plane is the follow-up); the 2D `handleMouseMove` bails in 3D (the hover-coords chip showed garbage); the idle agent-hover pick is rAF-throttled + canvas-rect-gated (was a full pick-FBO render + synchronous readPixels stall per raw window-wide pointermove).
- **Model lifecycle / UX**: `REMOVE_AGENT_MAPPING` cascades `clearDeletedId('mappingId')` (dead compiled pass + dangling picker); `UPDATE_AGENT_ATTRIBUTE` gained the Compare-tag `_port_x/y/y2` remap + a type-change linked-agent-mapping palette reset + `agentVariables` initialValue remap / tag-detach (REMOVE detaches too); `REORDER_AGENT_ATTRIBUTES` reducer + panel wiring (the drag handle was a silent no-op; a reorder forces the full reinit via `attrsStructurallyEqual`); `ADD_AGENT_MAPPING` seeds Standalone when no eligible agent attribute exists; the agent seed-config merge iterates `model.agentAttributes` keyed on `agentAttrSig` (it iterated CELL attrs — persisted seed config never survived a reload and cell edits wiped it); agent-graph compile errors surface in the compile-error banner (`[agents] …` — "No Behaviour Step node" was console-only) and agent OM compile errors are collected into `compileAgentGraph().error` instead of swallowed; the panel-drag drop menu applies `isNodeAvailable` (it offered lattice-only/async-only nodes the other menus hide); `behaviourStep`/`divisionEvent`/`agentInit` are singleton-enforced like Step/Init (`SINGLETON_NODE_TYPES` covers add/connect/paste/quick-add — a duplicate root was silently dead); a cross-graph clipboard renders a DISABLED "Paste (from X graph)" item instead of a silent no-op; Seed Pattern (compact/scatter) + Bond Rest Length got Properties rows; agent views got a Description field (feeds the viewer-tab tooltip) + "+ Add Agent View" works with zero agent attrs (Standalone); the Variables panel no longer spuriously auto-selects on a Cells↔Agents swap and agent tag variables bind AGENT tag attributes; `neighborIndex` is hidden from the agent-attribute type dropdown; `Neighborhood3DEditor` remounts per selection (`key={selected.id}` — stale form state overwrote neighbourhood B with A's spec); a glyph setCellLooks in a 3D model gets a validation badge (glyphs aren't rendered by the voxel view).
- **Parity-harness discipline**: [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)'s `buildArgs` mirror was missing the 3 sprite args (every trailing arg shifted — `_field_*` landed undefined), masking real-looking failures. The agent-loop ABI now has THREE mirrors: `buildAgentLoopParams` (compile.ts) ↔ `buildAgentLoopArgs` (sim.worker.ts) ↔ the harness `buildArgs` — edit ALL THREE together.
- **Agent `sequence` emitters walked NONEXISTENT ports** (found via a user gravitation model): both agent compilers' `case 'sequence'` walked `then0`/`then1` keyed on a nonexistent `sequenceCount` — the real ports are **`first`/`then`/`then_2`…** keyed on `extraCount` — so ANY Sequence in an agent behaviour silently dropped its ENTIRE downstream chain on WASM AND WebGPU (JS was fine → "works on JS only"). Sneaky because the gate/reachability walk the EDGE MAP (prefix scan), so every node still gate-passed + compiled — only the emitter's flow walk missed. Fixed in both; the parity harness's synthetic model now routes its agent chain through a Sequence for permanent regression coverage. **When adding a flow construct to the agent compilers, copy the port ids from the NODE DEF / asyncWriteHazard's walk — never guess.** No shipped sample used an agent-graph Sequence, which is why 9/9 parity passed while user models broke — sample coverage ≠ catalogue coverage for FLOW shapes.
- **WebGPU-agent perf posture surfaced in the UI**: the Properties WebGPU agent radio hint now states the per-step CPU↔GPU upload/readback cost (agents + cell fields) — below ~10k agents, and especially for field models (Ant Necrophoresis / Chemotaxis), JS/WASM is usually FASTER; WebGPU wins only at very large populations / very heavy per-agent math. This is the documented benchmark reality, not a bug.
- **Agents-only world resize at scale (the 600×600×400 hang)** — an agents-only (Grid Cells OFF) 3D model froze on resize to large volumes and never concluded, on every target. FOUR compounding worker causes, all fixed in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts):
  1. **The nbr-table reservation** — `computeMemoryLayout` reserved per-cell neighbour index tables (`total × nSz × 4` bytes; a Moore-3D nbh at 600×600×400 = 144M cells ≈ 15 GB, past the wasm32 4 GiB address space) even though `gridCellsEnabled === false` means `buildNeighborIndices` + the cell step never run. `initGrid` now passes `gridCellsEnabled ? neighborhoods : []` into the layout, so an agents-only world allocates only attrs/colors/scratch. Also wrapped `initGrid()` in the init handler with try/catch → a genuinely-too-big world posts a clear `Grid allocation failed for W×H×D…` error instead of a silent dead worker.
  2. **Static colours shipped every step** — with no colour pass, the (huge) unchanged `colors` buffer (144M cells × 4 B ≈ 576 MB/step) was copied + transferred on every `stepped`. New module flag `colorsDirty`: set by `runStep`, `writeDefaultColors`, a mapping fn actually running in `runColorPass`, the WebGPU colours readback, and the loadState restore; when `!gridCellsEnabled && !colorsDirty`, `sendColors` posts the stepped message WITHOUT `colors` (agents payload + indicators still ride). SimulatorView's stepped handler keeps the last buffer when `msg.colors === undefined`. Grid-ON models are untouched (the skip requires `!gridCellsEnabled`). The post-batch `runColorPass()` is also gated on `gridCellsEnabled` (2 sites) — the cell graph of an agents-only model still has an (empty) Step root, so `stepFn` exists and used to re-dirty the colours every batch.
  3. **`generation` never advanced** — the batch loops only incremented via `runStep`, so an agents-only model was stuck at gen 0 (end conditions, the transport display, and "is it even running?" all broken). All 3 batch loops (sync, webgpu-agents, webgpu-grid) now `generation++` when agents stepped but the cell step was skipped.
  4. **Lattice-target instantiation ran with a mismatched layout** — the lattice WASM module / WebGPU runtime bake nbr offsets the worker layout no longer reserves. `init` + `recompile` + `setUseWasm`/`setUseWebGPU` now gate lattice-target bring-up on `gridCellsEnabled` (agents-only runs the agent target only; the AGENT WASM/WebGPU targets are unaffected — their layout is separate).
  Verified live at 600×600×400 gridCells:false: JS agents gen 0→20 at **6.75 ms/step**, WASM agent target gen 0→20, 0 per-step colours ships, 0 errors (was: frozen forever); grid-ON regression checks — WASM GoL ships colours 5/5 steps gens 1..5, WebGPU GoL direct-render unchanged.
- **Agents-only per-cell layout eliminated entirely (round 2 — the 500³ / 5000×500×500 failure).** Even after the nbr-table skip, an agents-only world still reserved **9 bytes/cell of inert engine regions** — colors (4) + the always-reserved async orderArray (4) + the markCellUpdated skip flag (1) — so 5000×500×500 (1.25e9 cells ≈ 11.25 GB) threw `WebAssembly.Memory(): value 171662 is above the upper bound 65536`. (The error names WebAssembly on ALL targets because **`wasmMemory` is the single backing store every target's JS views sit on** — it's the container allocation, not the WASM compile target.) Fix: `computeMemoryLayout` ([wasm/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm/layout.ts)) gained a trailing `gridCells = true` param — when false, the colors / glyph / order / skipped regions are 0 bytes, the sync attr-WRITE region aliases the read region, and initGrid also drops the variegated orientation inputs; **per-cell memory for an agents-only world = its CELL ATTRIBUTES only** (kept — the agent field bridge reads/deposits through `readAttrs`; the shipped agent samples have zero cell attrs post-migration, so cost ≈ 0). Compile-side callers (`computeLayoutFromModel`) never pass the param → lattice layouts byte-identical. Worker guards: `writeDefaultColors`/`runStep`/`runColorPass` early-return when `!gridCellsEnabled` (mutation-handler tails could otherwise loop a huge `total`); the order/skipped views + `colorsDirty` are grid-gated; `sendColors` also skips on `colors.length === 0`; the allocation-failure message now explains the shared backing store + the 4 GiB cap + (grid-off) "per-cell storage is the cell attributes only". **SimulatorView renders agents WITHOUT a colors buffer**: the 3D draw no longer early-returns on `colorsRef === null` (voxel upload skipped, stale instances zeroed via `r.instanceCount = 0`), and the 2D draw's `!colors` early-return is waived for agent models (blit + glyph-fallback ImageData guarded by `colors.length >= w*h*4`). Verified live: 500³ AND **5000×500×500 (1.25 BILLION cells)** grid-off run gen 0→20, agents render (5 sphere instances, 0 voxels), 0 errors; grid-ON regressions clean (WASM GoL colours 5/5, async Amphiphile 5/5, Ant Necrophoresis grid+agents+field 5/5); parity harness 0 mismatches.

### Out of scope / deferred
Phase E (3D agent RENDERER — extend `gl3d.ts` to instanced spheres + bond tubes; the 3D agent COMPUTE runs on JS/WASM/WebGPU). The WebGPU agent **3D field bridge** + **glyph setCellLooks** are now DONE (see "PR7 FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT"); the remaining WebGPU agent rejects are the genuine fundamentals — `stopEvent` on the agent behaviour (a GPU stopFlag round-trip; no sample uses it), and `aggregate`/`groupOperator` median+uniform-random + `updateIndicator` toggle/next/prev (the same WebGPU fundamentals as the lattice grid — all of which DO run on the WASM agent target, which is f64+sequential). The **WASM-agent 3D field bridge** (trilinear) is now DONE — a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with JS bit-parity (see "Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE"). The WASM-agent field bridge (2D + 3D) + division-event[JS-on-CPU] + array vars + the whole catalogue are DONE (Tissue/Chemotaxis/everything run on WASM with JS bit-parity — see "Phase F — WASM AGENT WHOLE-TARGET PORT"). PR7c zero-copy/atomics (a perf optimisation, not a coverage gap — the benchmark shows the agent engine is O(N) via the spatial hash so the loop is not a JS bottleneck; the WebGPU upload/readback overhead means it's a wash-or-regression below ~10k agents). ~~The `.gcastate` FILE-format agent persistence~~ — DONE in the production-hardening pass above. A layer-aware 3D region copy/paste (Ctrl+C/V/X is 2D-only for now). A graph-authorable per-PAIR force curve (Apply Force authors the NET per-agent force today, not the pairwise law). A strict deposit-snapshot for the closed feedback (the fused pass mixes same-step deposits intra-loop — a small order-dependence, harmless for diffusion-driven models).

## PWA / Offline Support (installable app + Tauri scaffold)

GenesisCA ships as an installable, offline-capable Progressive Web App (the main install path), with a Tauri v2 shell that builds a standalone portable native exe (no installer — for ad-hoc / USB sharing). Decisions + full subsystem analysis: [docs/IMPACT_MAP_PWA_INSTALL.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_PWA_INSTALL.md) + [docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.md](docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.md) (illustrated mockup: `docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.html`).

### Build wiring ([vite.config.ts](vite.config.ts))
- `vite-plugin-pwa@1.3.0` (Workbox 7), **`generateSW`** strategy — NOT a hand-written service worker (Workbox revisions the precache from the real build output, so Vite's content hashing can't desync it; a hand-written SW would break on every deploy). Registered LAST in the plugins array so the library plugins' `closeBundle` (`models`/`macros` `index.json` + `*.thumb.*`) runs before the SW precache glob.
- **Base-path is the load-bearing detail** (§B of the impact map). `const base = process.env.GHPAGES ? '/GenesisCA/' : '/'`. The production site is served at the ROOT of the custom domain **genesisca.online** (pinned by [public/CNAME](public/CNAME), which GitHub Pages serves at the apex root), so the deploy now builds with base `/` — [deploy.yml](.github/workflows/deploy.yml) NO LONGER sets `GHPAGES`. `GHPAGES` is an opt-in fallback that rebuilds under the `/GenesisCA/` subpath, needed ONLY if the custom domain is removed and the bare `rff255.github.io/GenesisCA/` project URL is used directly (with the domain set, that URL 301-redirects to genesisca.online). Everything else already used `/`: `npm run dev`, a local `npm run build` + `npm run preview` (both serve at `http://localhost:<port>/`), and the Tauri native shell (its webview loads `dist/` from `tauri://localhost/` at root; release.yml does NOT set GHPAGES). The manifest `id`/`start_url`/`scope` + every icon `src` are built from `base` (`buildManifest(base)`); `index.html` icon links use `%BASE_URL%`. (The `GHPAGES` flag itself replaced an earlier `command === 'build'` + `TAURI_ENV_PLATFORM` heuristic — it's explicit and keeps local preview painless.)
- **Precache vs runtime cache — keep the precache LEAN.** A couple of library `.gcaproj` embed large saved sim-states (Elementary CA 1D ≈ 10.8 MB, Gray-Scott ≈ 5 MB), so `globPatterns` precaches only the app shell + `sim.worker` + icons + `models/index.json` + `macros/index.json` + macros + thumbnails (~33 entries, ~8.6 MB — the animated-GIF thumbnails dominate). Model `.gcaproj` files are NOT precached; they're runtime-cached `StaleWhileRevalidate` on first open (offline thereafter). **Overriding `globPatterns` REPLACES Workbox's default `**/*.{js,css,html}`** — the shell MUST be re-listed. `maximumFileSizeToCacheInBytes` is a guard: the build ERRORS if a glob-matched file exceeds it rather than silently dropping it (this is how the oversized models were caught). The `models/`+`macros/` globs are gated on `command === 'build'` — they only exist in `dist/`, not `dev-dist/`, so including them under `vite dev` makes Workbox warn "pattern doesn't match any files". (The shell glob `**/*.{js,css,html,…}` is always present and doesn't warn in dev.)
- `devOptions: { enabled: true }`: the SW runs under `vite dev` too, so install / offline are testable straight from `npm run dev` at `http://localhost:5173/` — no `npm run preview` needed. (Combined with the build-only library globs above, dev is warning-free.)
- `registerType: 'autoUpdate'`: a new deploy applies silently on the next load. No "update available" prompt — there's no in-app update channel and the app does no online processing, so a toast would be noise.

### App wiring ([src/App.tsx](src/App.tsx))
- `registerSW({ immediate: true })` from `virtual:pwa-register` (types via `/// <reference types="vite-plugin-pwa/client" />` in [src/vite-env.d.ts](src/vite-env.d.ts)) — **registered on the WEB ONLY**, NO callbacks: the offline-ready and update toasts were removed as noise (the app always works offline; updates auto-apply). The only remaining toast is the load-confirmation one (`showToast` in App.tsx, plain `string`, 3.5 s auto-dismiss).
- **CRITICAL — never run the service worker in the Tauri native shell.** In WebView2 the SW caches the whole app into the webview's PERSISTENT profile and then serves that STALE copy on every launch, so a freshly-built exe shows the OLD UI / models / version no matter what it actually embeds (this caused the v1.19.x "stale exe" saga — the exe was always correct; the cached SW was overriding it; the web PWA was unaffected because browsers refresh SWs). So App.tsx's mount effect branches on `'__TAURI_INTERNALS__' in window`: **web** → `registerSW()` + `navigator.storage.persist()`; **native** → unregister any SW (`serviceWorker.getRegistrations().then(r=>r.unregister())`) + clear `caches`, and do NOT register. The native build embeds + serves its own assets, so it never needs a SW. **Debug aid:** if a machine already has the stale SW cached, the new exe alone can't always clear it (the old SW may serve the old bundle before the new code runs) — delete `%LOCALAPPDATA%\io.github.rff255.genesisca\EBWebView\Default\Service Worker` (kill lingering `msedgewebview2.exe` processes for that profile first, or the folder is locked) to break the loop once.
- `navigator.storage?.persist?.()` on mount — auto-granted once installed; protects the offline cache from LRU eviction. **Storage durability only — does NOT raise the memory ceiling** for large grids (renderer heap / WASM32 4 GiB address space). That ceiling is identical in the current Tauri shell (it wraps the same Chromium/WebView2 engine) — only moving the simulation to native Rust would raise it; the native build's real wins are storage/filesystem + a clean window, not compute.
- **Boot splash** ([index.html](index.html) → removed in [App.tsx](src/App.tsx)): a static `#splash` overlay (inline `<style>` + two-cell mark + wordmark + shimmer) painted the instant the page parses so there's no white-screen gap before React mounts; `html`/`body` are set to `#0c0d10` for the same reason (manifest `background_color` covers the OS-launch frame). App.tsx's mount effect adds `.splash-hide` then removes `#splash` after a ~350 ms fade. Pure static HTML/CSS (no bundle dep) — don't let the theme boot `<script>` clobber it. It self-removes, so to screenshot it, re-inject the markup via `preview_eval`.
- [InstallButton.tsx](src/components/InstallButton.tsx) (rendered last in the navbar RIGHT group): captures `beforeinstallprompt` (`preventDefault` + stash), renders the amber "⤓ Install" pill ONLY while installable, fires the native prompt on click, and self-hides on `appinstalled` (no toast — the button vanishing IS the feedback). The shared navbar icon-button style was lifted out of App.tsx to [navStyles.ts](src/components/navStyles.ts) (also used by the keyboard-shortcuts button).
- **No window-controls-overlay (evaluated + dropped).** WCO can't auto-hide the installed PWA's title bar — Chromium shows it by default and only adds a per-app "hide title bar" toggle the user must click (sticky per app). That fails the zero-effort bar, so the PWA stays plain `display: 'standalone'` (own window + icon, no tabs/address bar, but a thin browser title bar remains). The toggle-free clean native header is the **Tauri** build instead (its own OS window). Don't re-add `display_override: ['window-controls-overlay', …]` expecting an automatic clean header — it only surfaces that manual toggle. (The matching `@media (display-mode: window-controls-overlay)` CSS block was removed too.)

### Icons ([public/](public/))
- Source mark: [public/icon.svg](public/icon.svg) — the two-cell (first-cleavage) mark on a full-bleed Nocturne `#0c0d10` tile, cells sized to sit inside the maskable safe circle. Doubles as the scalable SVG favicon.
- `@vite-pwa/assets-generator` ([pwa-assets.config.ts](pwa-assets.config.ts), `npm run generate-pwa-assets`) emits `pwa-{64,192,512}x*.png`, `maskable-icon-512x512.png`, `apple-touch-icon-180x180.png`, `favicon.ico` (maskable/apple backgrounds overridden to `#0c0d10` so no white halo). **The generated icons are COMMITTED** — CI's `npm run build` does not regenerate them; rerun the script if `icon.svg` changes.
- `npx tauri icon public/icon.svg` emits the native set into `src-tauri/icons/` (`.ico`/`.icns`/PNGs + Windows-store/Android/iOS).

### Native shell ([src-tauri/](src-tauri/), Tauri v2)
- Wraps the same `dist/` in the OS webview; `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle` → a **standalone portable `genesisca.exe`** (the Cargo package name, in `target/release/`) — NO installer. `bundle.active:false` in `tauri.conf.json` (installer bundling intentionally dropped — the PWA is the main install path; the exe is just for ad-hoc/USB sharing); `bundle.icon` is kept so the exe still embeds the GenesisCA icon. Also in `tauri.conf.json`: `frontendDist:'../dist'`, `beforeBuildCommand:'npm run build'`, identifier `io.github.rff255.genesisca`, and **`version:'../package.json'`** so the exe version always matches the web app — package.json is the single source of truth; do NOT hardcode a version in tauri.conf.json (it's no longer one of the version-bump touch points).
- **Release CI** ([.github/workflows/release.yml](.github/workflows/release.yml)): on every `v*` tag (or manual dispatch), `windows-latest` + Rust runs `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle`, renames `target/release/*.exe` → `GenesisCA-<tag>.exe`, and publishes it to a GitHub Release via `softprops/action-gh-release` (NOT `tauri-action` — that uploads installer *bundles*, and we ship a raw exe). **The job runs a CLEAN build — NO `swatinem/rust-cache`** — on purpose: Tauri embeds the web frontend INTO the binary at compile time (`generate_context!`), so a restored `target/` cache can let cargo skip the recompile that re-embeds it and ship a STALE UI in the exe even when the source is current (the v1.19.0 build hit exactly this — correct source, stale embedded frontend; Pages is immune since it rebuilds `dist/` fresh). Releases are infrequent, so the extra compile time is worth the guarantee. Pages (`deploy.yml`) still deploys on every master push; since the version only bumps at release, the online app and the latest exe always report the same version. Download link for users: `https://github.com/rff255/GenesisCA/releases/latest` (unsigned → first run shows a SmartScreen "More info → Run anyway" prompt). The job needs repo **Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions = "Read and write"** so its `GITHUB_TOKEN` can create the Release (else it 403s). **Building needs the Rust toolchain (`rustup`)** + WebView2 (preinstalled Win10/11); the committed `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` carries the `alloc-stdlib` 0.2.2 pin that fixes a transient brotli build break (keep it). `src-tauri/target/` + `gen/` are gitignored; the rest is committed.
- **Native Save As (implemented):** in the Tauri shell the browser blob-download (`<a download>`) is silently dropped by WebView2, so [fileOperations.ts](src/model/fileOperations.ts)'s `saveTextFile` branches on `'__TAURI_INTERNALS__' in window` → `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `save()` (real OS Save As dialog) + a custom `save_text_file` Rust command (`std::fs::write`, in [src-tauri/src/lib.rs](src-tauri/src/lib.rs)) to write the chosen path. App-defined commands need no ACL; the dialog plugin needs `dialog:default` in `capabilities/default.json` (+ `tauri-plugin-dialog` in Cargo.toml, `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` in package.json). `downloadJSON`/`downloadStateFile` are now `async → Promise<boolean>` (false = user cancelled → FileMenu skips `markSaved`). Load still uses the browser `<input type=file>` (WebView2 opens the native picker natively). The browser/PWA path is unchanged (blob download to the Downloads folder).
- Remaining future escape from browser storage limits: move bulk `.gcaproj`/`.gcastate` storage onto `tauri-plugin-fs` (no quota). WebGPU is only guaranteed on the Windows WebView2/Chromium build; macOS/Linux Tauri use WebKit → the simulator falls back to the WASM/JS targets.

### Manifest is intentionally version-less
PWA install identity is `id`/`start_url`, and the SW precache is content-hash-revisioned by Workbox — so a version bump drives neither. The `/updateversion` four-file sweep is unchanged; do NOT add a `version` field to the manifest.
